[^1455]: Sessional Hymn after the second Stichology, Tone 5 (repeated for *Glory... Physical Leaf p461 (printed p. 455 / fol. 455r).

[^1470]: Sessional Hymn of the Forefeast of the Ascension, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): Christ's voluntary nativity, manifestation, Passion, Resurrection, and glorious Ascension elevating and glorifying human nature. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1471]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 4: Heirmos (*Бж҃твенное твоѐ разꙋмѣ́въ* / *Perceiving Thy divine condescension*), Habakkuk contemplating the divine exhaustion and crying aloud: *Glory to Thy power, O Lord!* (Hab. 3:2). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1472]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, first troparion [Acrostic letter H]: human nature, formerly condemned by sin, receiving divine compassion through Christ's assumption of flesh and hymning His Resurrection and Ascension with fear. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1473]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, second troparion [Acrostic letter Th]: the incorporeal ranks marveling as they behold Christ bearing earthly flesh, ascending upon a cloud, and mounting unto the heavenly heights. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1474]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, third troparion [Acrostic letter I] (preceded by *Glory*): the angelic powers awestruck at the sight of Christ's crimson garments as He ascends, bowing down in mingled fear and joy. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1475]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 4, fourth troparion [Acrostic letter K] (preceded by *Both now*): summoning creation to clap hands as the risen Lord ascends into heaven and the Angels submit to Him as Creator and God. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1476]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 4: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Оу҆слы́шахъ слꙋ́хъ си́лы Крⷭ҇та̀* / *I heard the report of the power of the Cross*). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1477]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 5: Heirmos (*Ѡ҆дѣѧ́йсѧ свѣ́томъ* / *O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light*), the prophet rising early to seek the Lord Who is clothed with light as with a garment (Ps. 103:2 LXX). Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1478]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 5, first troparion [Acrostic letter L] (chanted twice): Christ abolishing the ancient condemnation of humanity, rising from the dead, and ascending to bestow the honor of session beside the Father upon those He loved. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1479]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 5, second troparion [Acrostic letter M] (preceded by *Glory*): Christ gladdening His Disciples after the Resurrection and ascending unto the Father from Whom He was never separated, even while conversing with mortals on earth. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1480]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 5, third troparion [Acrostic letter N] (preceded by *Both now*): Christ fulfilling the predictions of the Law and the prophetic proclamations, ascending upon a receiving cloud unto the heavens. Physical Leaf p463 (printed p. 457 / fol. 457r).

[^1481]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 5: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Оу҆́тренююще вопіе́мъ тѝ* / *Rising early we cry unto Thee*). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1482]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 6: Heirmos (*Неи́стовствꙋющеесѧ бу́рею* / *Raging with a tempest*), the soul tossed by the tempest of passions praying to be brought up from corruption like Jonah from the sea monster (Jonah 2:3–7). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1483]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 6, first troparion [Acrostic letter X] (chanted twice): Christ appearing as a strange sight unto the Angels, bearing the form of a man in His awesome ascent. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1484]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 6, second troparion [Acrostic letter O] (preceded by *Glory*): the Lord of all ascending in radiant glory unto His unoriginate Father while all creation keeps festival. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1485]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 6, third troparion [Acrostic letter P] (preceded by *Both now*): all nations called to cry aloud as Christ ascends with a shout and the sound of a divine trumpet unto the heavens, which He never abandoned. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1486]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 6: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Ѡ҆бы́де мѧ̀ бе́здна* / *The abyss hath compassed me*). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1487]: Rubric for the Kontakion and Ikos of Pascha after Ode 6 (*Though Thou didst descend into the grave...*), referencing printed fol. 16 (Physical Leaf p22), followed by the reading of the Synaxarion from the Menaion. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1488]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 7: Heirmos (*Превозноси́мый Ѻ҆тце́въ* / *The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers*), praising the God of the fathers Who quenched the fiery flame for the Three Holy Youths in Babylon (Dan. 3:26). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1489]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, first troparion [Acrostic letter R]: Christ tearing asunder the middle wall of enmity by His Cross and Passion (Eph. 2:14), borne upon a cloud into the presence of His unoriginate Father. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1490]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, second troparion [Acrostic letter S]: the heavenly powers crying in trembling for the eternal gates to be lifted up (Ps. 23:7 LXX) as Christ arrives in earthly flesh having vanquished Beliar. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1491]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, third troparion [Acrostic letter T] (preceded by *Glory*): the divine hosts questioning why the Savior's robes are dyed crimson, and Christ answering that He has trodden the winepress alone (Isa. 63:1–3). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1492]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 7, fourth troparion [Acrostic letter Y] (preceded by *Both now*): the faithful harmoniously praising Christ's Crucifixion and Rising, and radiantly celebrating His venerable Ascension unto all ages. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1493]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 7: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Въ пещѝ ѻ҆́гненнѣй* / *In the fiery furnace*). Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1494]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 8: Heirmos (*Тебѣ̀ всесодѣ́телю* / *Unto Thee, the Fashioner of all*), calling the children in the furnace to sing and exalt the Creator of all unto the ages. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1495]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, first troparion [Acrostic letter Ph]: a radiant cloud receiving Christ, the Light of Light, from the Mount of Olives before the gaze of His divine Disciples. Physical Leaf p464 (printed p. 458 / fol. 458r).

[^1496]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, second troparion [Acrostic letter Ch]: clapping hands in psalmody as Christ our God rises and ascends to the place whence He descended in divine love for mankind, establishing peace. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1497]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, third troparion [Acrostic letter Ps] (preceded by *Let us bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord*): King David proclaiming Christ ascending upon the Cherubim as upon wings of spiritual hosts (Ps. 17:11 LXX). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1498]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 8, fourth troparion [Acrostic letter O] (preceded by *Both now*): Christ appearing to the Disciples after His Resurrection, leading them to the Mount of Olives, and being lifted up on a radiant cloud into the heights. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1499]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 8: Heirmos of the Ascension (*И҆з̾ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ пре́жде вѣ́къ* / *Begotten of the Father before the ages*). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1500]: Rubric omitting the Magnificat (*More honorable than the Cherubim...*) and prescribing the chanting of the Paschal refrains for Ode 9. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1501]: Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5, Ode 9: Heirmos (*И҆са́їе ликꙋ́й* / *Rejoice, O Isaiah*), the prophetic vision of the Virgin conceiving and bearing Emmanuel, God and Man (Isa. 7:14). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1502]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, first troparion: all creation celebrating Christ's Ascension, wherein He presented the human nature He assumed unto the Father in an unchangeable mystery. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1503]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, second troparion: Christ breaking the bars of hades, rising from the dead, and ascending to the heavenly ranks who command the eternal gates to open. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1504]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, third troparion: the Angels appearing to the disciples on the Mount of Olives, testifying that Christ Who ascended shall return in glory to judge the living and the dead (Acts 1:11). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1505]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, fourth troparion (preceded by *Glory*): prayer for Christ to bestow His divine peace upon the faithful, preserving all in mutual love even as He blessed His disciples of old. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1506]: Canon of the Forefeast, Ode 9, Theotokion (preceded by *Both now*): praising the immaculate Virgin as the light-bearing chariot of the noetic Sun, bringing the light of divine knowledge to those sitting in darkness. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1507]: Katavasia rubric for Ode 9: Heirmos of the Ascension (*Тѧ̀ па́че ᲂу҆ма̀* / *Thee who art beyond understanding*). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1508]: Rubric for the Exapostilaria: the Paschal Exapostilarion (*Having fallen asleep in the flesh...*) chanted twice, followed by the Exapostilarion of the Blind Man (*My spiritual eyes...*), referencing printed fol. 440 (Physical Leaf p446). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1509]: Rubric for the Praises (*Ainoi*, Pss. 148–150 LXX), appointing 8 verses and 4 automelic stichera of the Blind Man. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1510]: First Automelic Sticheron of the Blind Man at the Praises, Tone 2: the monologue of the man born blind, questioning whether parental sin caused his blindness and imploring Christ for mercy. Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1511]: Second Automelic Sticheron of the Blind Man at the Praises, Tone 2: Jesus departing the temple, making clay with spittle to anoint the blind man's eyes, and sending him to wash in Siloam, leading to his bold confession of Christ the Messiah before his neighbors (Jn. 9:1–11). Physical Leaf p465 (printed p. 459 / fol. 459r).

[^1512]: Third and Fourth Automelic Stichera of the Blind Man at the Praises (Tone 4: *Considering his whole life as night...*; Tone 5: *O Lord, as Thou wast passing by along the way...*), referenced from Tuesday Vespers. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1513]: Rubric for the Stichera of Pascha with their psalm refrains (*Let God arise...*), referencing printed fol. 305 (Physical Leaf p311), followed by the Doxastikon in Tone 5 (*It is the day of Resurrection...*) and *Christ is risen from the dead...* sung once with melody. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1514]: Rubric for the Great Doxology, the Resurrection Troparion in Tone 5 (*The Word co-unoriginate*), its Theotokion, the litany, and the dismissal without the Cross commemorating the saint of the day. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1515]: Rubric for the First Hour celebrated in the narthex, reciting the customary psalms, Troparion, and Theotokion (*What shall we call thee, O full of grace...*). Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1516]: Rubric concluding the First Hour with the Kontakion of the Blind Man (*With spiritual eyes...*) and the final dismissal, and prescribing the Third, Sixth, and Ninth Hours before Liturgy to be chanted with their customary three psalms, alternating the Resurrection and Paschal Kontakia. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1517]: Rubric for the solemn opening of the Divine Liturgy celebrated exactly as on Easter Sunday itself, with the priest chanting the Paschal verses before the altar with the censer and paschal candle. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1518]: Rubric for the Divine Liturgy on the Apodosis of Pascha: appointing the Typika (Pss. 102 and 145 LXX with the Beatitudes), Odes 3 and 6 of the Canon, the Resurrection Troparion, Kontakia of the Blind Man and Pascha, the Paschal Prokeimenon (*This is the day which the Lord hath made*, Ps. 117:24 LXX), Alleluia, Epistle and Gospel of the day, and Communion Hymn (*Receive the Body of Christ*) with Psalm 33 (Ps. 34 LXX). Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1519]: Concluding Liturgy rubric for the solemn dismissal of Pascha with the blessing of the Holy Cross, sealing the forty-day liturgical celebration of the Feast of Pascha. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1520]: Trapeza rubric permitting a festive meal with oil, fish, and wine for the monastic brethren on the Apodosis of Pascha before the Great Feast of the Ascension. Physical Leaf p466 (printed p. 460 / fol. 460r).

[^1521]: Major feast day headpiece: Feast of the Ascension of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ (*ВОЗНЕСЕ́НЇЕ Го́спода Бо́га и҆ Спа́са на́шегѡ І҆исоу́са Хрїста́*), celebrated on Thursday of the Sixth Week after Pascha (forty days after the Resurrection). Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1522]: Rubric for the Ninth Hour on Wednesday evening, prescribing the Trisagion to be chanted at the beginning of all liturgical offices until Pentecost Sunday, ending the Paschal season's replacement of introductory prayers. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1523]: Rubric for Small Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension, appointing 4 stichera at *Lord, I have cried*. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1524]: First Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (*Нб҃нымъ чинѡ́мъ*): Christ renewing creation through His incarnation and ascending in flesh to sit at the right hand of the Father, awaiting to return as Judge. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1525]: Second Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: angels addressing the Apostles on the Mount of Olives, declaring that Christ Who ascended shall return as Judge to render unto each according to their works (Acts 1:11). Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1526]: Third Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: petition for the ascended Savior to illumine the hearts of the faithful with divine grace even as He bestowed heavenly glory upon the witnesses of His Ascension. Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1527]: Doxastikon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): exhortation for the assembly of the faithful to join the Apostles on the Mount of Olives and chant with David: *God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet* (Ps. 46:5 LXX). Physical Leaf p467 (printed p. 461 / fol. 461r).

[^1528]: Rubric for the Stichera at the Aposticha of Small Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1529]: First Aposticha Sticheron, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (*До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ*): marveling at the exaltation of human nature, which ascended into the heavens united hypostatically unto the divine Word. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1530]: Psalm verse at the Aposticha: *Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing* (Ps. 46:1 LXX [Ps. 47:1]). Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1531]: Second Aposticha Sticheron, Tone 2: the radiant day of the Master's divine ascent into the heavens shining forth to enlighten all creation. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1532]: Psalm verse at the Aposticha: *God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet* (Ps. 46:5 LXX [Ps. 47:5]). Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1533]: Third Aposticha Sticheron, Tone 2: petition to Christ to bestow His divine grace upon the Church even as He sent the consubstantial Holy Spirit unto the Disciples from on high. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1534]: Doxastikon at the Aposticha, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): the Most Holy Mother of God beholding her Son borne upon the clouds and offering divine praise together with the Apostles. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1535]: Rubric for the conclusion of Small Vespers: the Prayer of St. Simeon (*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart*), the Trisagion prayers, the Lord's Prayer, and the Dismissal Troparion of the Ascension. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1536]: Dismissal Troparion of the Ascension, Tone 4: *Thou hast ascended in glory, O Christ our God...*, proclaiming the gladdening of the Disciples by the promise of the Holy Spirit and the confirmation of Christ as the Son of God and Redeemer of the world. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1537]: Rubric concluding Small Vespers with the Little Litany and the priestly dismissal. Physical Leaf p468 (printed p. 462 / fol. 462r).

[^1538]: Woodcut plate depicting the Ascension of our Lord Jesus Christ: Christ ascending within a mandorla of light and clouds, His hands extended in blessing, attended by angels; below, the Most Holy Theotokos standing in prayer with the Holy Apostles looking upward in wonder on the Mount of Olives; framed by elaborate Baroque floral scrollwork borders. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1539]: Heading and opening rubric for Great Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension, prescribing the omission of the Kathisma psalmody following the Introductory Psalm (Ps. 103 LXX). Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1540]: Rubric for *Lord, I have cried*, appointing 10 verses and 5 automelic stichera chanted twice each. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1541]: First Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (chanted twice): Christ ascending into heaven to send the Paraclete unto the world, the angels marveling at seeing human flesh exalted above themselves, and the Holy Spirit bidding the angels open the eternal gates. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1542]: Second Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (chanted twice): the Cherubim struck with wonder as they behold Christ, Who sits upon them, ascending upon the clouds. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1543]: Third Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (chanted twice): the faithful contemplating Christ's Ascension upon the holy mount, praising His countenance as the Brightness of the Father's glory, venerating His Passion, and honoring His Resurrection. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1544]: Fourth Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (chanted twice): the Apostles weeping as Christ is taken up into the clouds and imploring Him not to leave them orphans, but to send the promised Most Holy Spirit to illumine their souls. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1545]: Fifth Automelic Sticheron at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (chanted twice): Christ accomplishing the mystery of the dispensation on the Mount of Olives, traversing the heavenly firmament, and ascending unto the Father while promising to send the All-Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p469 (printed p. 463 / fol. 463r).

[^1546]: Doxastikon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): sweetest Jesus, inseparable from the Father's bosom, ascending from the Mount of Olives in glory and seating redeemed human nature beside the Father, while angelic powers marvel and the faithful pray to share in the joy of His elect. Physical Leaf p470 (printed p. 464 / fol. 464r).

[^1547]: Rubric for the solemn Entrance with the censer, the Prokeimenon of the day, and three Old Testament readings (Paroimiai) appointed for the Great Feast of the Ascension. Physical Leaf p470 (printed p. 464 / fol. 464r).

[^1548]: First Old Testament Reading: Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 2:2–3 LXX), the mountain of the Lord established upon the tops of the mountains in the last days, exalted above the hills, with all nations flowing unto it to learn the ways of God. Physical Leaf p470 (printed p. 464 / fol. 464r).

[^1549]: Second Old Testament Reading: Prophecy of Isaiah (Isa. 62:10–12; 63:1–3, 7–9 LXX), preparing the way for the Lord, the proclamation of the Savior coming to Sion, the warrior returning from Edom with crimson garments from Bosor having trodden the winepress alone, and the Lord saving His people through His own love and mercy. Physical Leaf p470 (printed p. 464 / fol. 464r).

[^1550]: Third Old Testament Reading: Prophecy of Zechariah (Zech. 14:4, 8–11 LXX), the Lord standing upon the Mount of Olives east of Jerusalem, living waters flowing forth to the eastern and western seas in summer and spring, the Lord reigning as King over all the earth, and Jerusalem dwelling safely without anathema. Physical Leaf p470 (printed p. 464 / fol. 464r).

[^1551]: Heading and opening rubric for the Litiya at Great Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension, appointing automelic stichera in Tone 1 and Tone 4. Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1552]: First Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 1: Christ ascending into heaven whence He descended, petitioned not to leave His disciples as orphans but to send the Holy Spirit to bring peace unto the world and manifest His divine power unto the sons of men. Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1553]: Second Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 1: Christ ascending unto His unoriginate Father without departing from His uncircumscribed bosom, receiving the ceaseless thrice-holy praise of the heavenly hosts, and remaining the only-begotten Son even in His incarnate state. Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1554]: Third Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 1: the Angels declaring unto the Apostles on the Mount of Olives that Christ Who ascended into heaven shall return in like manner, summoning all to serve Him in holiness and righteousness (Acts 1:11; Lk. 1:75). Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1555]: Fourth Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 4: Christ ascending from the Mount of Olives to accomplish the Father's good pleasure, causing awe among the Angels and terror in the abyss, while the disciples tremble with joy before the cloud prepared as an awaiting throne, heaven revealing its beauty, and the earth revealing its hidden treasures. Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1556]: Fifth Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 4: human nature renewed in Christ and exalted above all principalities and powers, seated with the Father through His co-suffering and impassible Passion, while the bodiless hosts marvel at the comely God-Man and Angels in white robes announce His second coming as Judge of living and dead (Acts 1:10–11). Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1557]: Sixth Automelic Sticheron at the Litiya, Tone 4: the clouds bearing Christ upward in His flesh before the eyes of the Disciples, the heavenly gates lifting up (Ps. 23:7 LXX), the angelic choir rejoicing, and the Disciples imploring the Good Shepherd to send the Most Holy Spirit to guide, confirm, enlighten, and sanctify their souls. Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1558]: Doxastikon at the Litiya, Tone 4 (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): Christ fulfilling the pre-eternal mystery on the Mount of Olives in the presence of His all-pure Mother, who having grieved more than all in His Passion as a mother, was meetly granted supreme joy in the glorification and heavenly ascension of His deified flesh. Physical Leaf p471 (printed p. 465 / fol. 465r).

[^1559]: Heading and rubric for the Stichera at the Aposticha of Great Vespers on the Eve of the Ascension, appointing three automelic stichera in Tone 2 with psalm verses from Psalm 46 LXX. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1560]: First Automelic Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2: Christ's voluntary Nativity, manifestation, Passion in the flesh, Resurrection, and Ascension into glory, filling all creation with His Godhead and sending the divine Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1561]: Psalm verse at the Aposticha: *Clap your hands, all ye nations; shout unto God with the voice of rejoicing* (Ps. 46:1 LXX [Ps. 47:1]). Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1562]: Second Automelic Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2: the heavenly Powers questioning the ascending Christ's identity and crimson vesture, invoking the prophetic vision of the warrior from Bosor (Isa. 63:1–3), and Christ sitting at the right hand of Majesty and sending the Holy Spirit to guide and save our souls. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1563]: Psalm verse at the Aposticha: *God is gone up with a shout, the Lord with the sound of a trumpet* (Ps. 46:5 LXX [Ps. 47:5]). Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1564]: Third Automelic Sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2: Christ ascending in glory upon the Mount of Olives before His Disciples, sitting at the right hand of the Father, and sending the Holy Spirit to enlighten, confirm, and sanctify the faithful. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1565]: Doxastikon at the Aposticha, Tone 6 (preceded by *Glory... Both now...*): Christ ascending with a shout and trumpet blast to lift up the fallen image of Adam and send the Holy Spirit, the Comforter, to sanctify our souls. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1566]: Rubric for the conclusion of Vespers: reciting the Prayer of St. Simeon (*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart*) and the Trisagion prayers through the Lord's Prayer. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1567]: Rubric for the blessing of loaves (*Artoklasia*): chanting the Troparion of the Ascension in Tone 4 (*Thou hast ascended in glory...*) thrice, followed by the great Patristic/Scriptural reading from the Acts of the Holy Apostles. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1568]: Typikon rubric for parishes or churches where no All-Night Vigil is celebrated: chanting the Troparion of the Feast once after *Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart*. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1569]: Rubric for Compline on the eve of the Ascension: chanting the Kontakion of the Feast after *It is truly right* and the Trisagion. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1570]: Rubric for the Midnight Office on the Feast of the Ascension: celebrated according to custom with the censer. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1571]: Rubric replacing the customary troparion *Behold, the Bridegroom comes* with the Troparion of the Ascension after the first Trisagion at the Midnight Office. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1572]: Rubric replacing the customary troparion *Remember, O Lord, for Thou art good* with the Kontakion of the Ascension after the second Trisagion at the Midnight Office. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1573]: Rubric concluding the Midnight Office with 40 repetitions of *Lord, have mercy* and the priestly dismissal. Physical Leaf p472 (printed p. 466 / fol. 466r).

[^1579]: Sessional Hymn after the second Stichology, Tone 3, Podoben: "Awed by the beauty of thy virginity" (*Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства*): the pre-eternal God deifying human nature and ascending in glory while Angels lead the way and announce His triumph to the Apostles. [^1965]: Ikos of the Holy Fathers: The Church crying aloud with the voice of wisdom (Jn. 7:37; Prov. 9:1–5), offering the chalice of orthodox confession and citing divine immutability (Mal. 3:6; Is. 44:6). Physical Leaf p507 (printed p. 501).

[^1966]: Title of the liturgical Synaxarion commemorating the 318 God-bearing Fathers of the First Ecumenical Council held at Nicaea in AD 325. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).

[^1967]: First poetic verse prefixed to the Synaxarion of the Fathers: Pleading with the conciliar assembly to illumine the mind with rays of divine wisdom. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).

[^1968]: Second poetic verse of the Synaxarion: Declaring that Arius, having alienated the Son from the Father's essence, became an alien to the glory of God. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).

[^1969]: Prose narrative of the Synaxarion for the Seventh Sunday after Pascha: Chronicling the historical background of the Council of Nicaea (AD 325), the rise of Arianism in Alexandria, the excommunication by St. Peter of Alexandria following his vision of Christ in a torn robe, the restoration by Achillas, the expulsion by St. Alexander, the theological arguments recorded by Theodoret, the convocation by St. Constantine the Great, the arrival of 318 delegates (including Sts. Sylvester, Metrophanes through locum tenentes, Alexander with Archdeacon Athanasius, Eustathius, Macarius, Paphnutius, Nicholas of Myra, and Spyridon of Trimythous), the drafting and signing of the Nicene Creed up to the Holy Spirit, the fixing of the Paschal computus, the promulgation of twenty canons, the miracle of the deceased bishops signing the decree, the dedication of Constantinople to the Mother of God, and the horrific death of Arius in a public latrine mirroring that of Judas. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).

[^1970]: Concluding prayer of the Synaxarion invoking the intercessions of the 318 Holy Fathers of Nicaea. Physical Leaf p508 (printed p. 502).

[^2169]: Third Sessional Hymn after the 1st stichology, Tone 6 (with Ps. 33:18 LXX): The commemoration of the holy martyrs blooming like the garden of Eden unto all creation. Physical Leaf p535 (printed p. 529).

[^2174]: Third and fourth stanzas of the Funeral Evlogetaria, Tone 5: Christ calling those who bore the cross to heavenly crowns, and the soul confessing itself as an image of divine glory wounded by sin. Physical Leaf p536 (printed p. 530).

[^2179]: Theotokion of the Sessional Hymn, Tone 5: Christ revealing believers as sons of light through His birth from the Virgin. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2183]: Troparion 3 of Ode 1, Tone 8: Human life as a fleeting shadow, with prayer that Orthodox priests, rulers, and all people be revealed as sons of unwaning day. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2184]: Troparion 4 of Ode 1, Tone 8: Commemoration of those who met death through water, war, earthquake, violence, or fire, placing them with the righteous. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2185]: Troparion 5 of Ode 1, Tone 8: Overlooking carnal debts and setting all departed of every age uncondemned before the dread tribunal of Christ. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2186]: Triadicon of Ode 1 (*Glory*), Tone 8: Confession of the three distinct, self-hypostatic Persons of the one uncreated Godhead. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2187]: Theotokion of Ode 1 (*Both now*), Tone 8: Mary as a mystical Heaven upon earth from whom the Sun of righteousness hath shone forth. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2188]: Ode 2 of the Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos (Canticle of Moses, Deut. 32:39): God the Word begotten before ages and born of the Virgin to destroy Adam's sin. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2189]: Troparion 1 of Ode 2, Tone 8: The divine proclamation fixing the boundaries of earthly life and receiving the faithful unto incorruption. Physical Leaf p537 (printed p. 531).

[^2190]: Troparion 2 of Ode 2, Tone 8: The Lord receiving the faithful dead from the four corners of the earth, whether in water, dry land, wilderness, or devoured by wild beasts. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2191]: Troparion 3 of Ode 2, Tone 8: God holding all creation in His palm, regathering the bodies dissolved into the four elements at the general resurrection. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2192]: Troparion 4 of Ode 2, Tone 8: The dread Second Coming of Christ like lightning, calling the faithful who went forth to meet Him to abide with Him for ever. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2194]: Theotokion of Ode 2 (*Both now*), Tone 8: The ineffable mystery of virgin motherhood known only to the supernatural Incarnate Word. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2195]: Ode 3 of the Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos: Prayer unto the Word of God Who established the heavens to confirm the hearts of believers. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2196]: Troparia 1-2 of Ode 3, Tone 8: The heavenly crown of the righteous and prayer for those suddenly snatched away by lightnings, frost, or sudden strokes. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2197]: Troparia 3-4 of Ode 3, Tone 8: Crossing the tempestuous sea of life to reach the haven of incorruption, and the resurrection of those devoured by sea creatures or birds. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2198]: Triadicon of Ode 3 (*Glory*), Tone 8: Theological contemplation of the divine Unity seen in three radiant Persons like the flash of lightning. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2199]: Theotokion of Ode 3 (*Both now*), Tone 8: Incomprehensible virginity preserved in childbirth, praised by angelic hosts and the race of men. Physical Leaf p538 (printed p. 532).

[^2200]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 5 (Melodic model: *The Word co-unoriginate*): Christ enduring death and overthrowing hades, besought to grant rest and kingdom unto the departed. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2201]: Sessional Theotokion (*Both now*), Tone 5: Marian supplication for speedy protection, calm amid turbulent thoughts, and the lifting up of a fallen soul. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2202]: Ode 4 of the Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos (Hab. 3:3): Habakkuk's vision of the Incarnate Word coming forth from the overshadowing mountain of the Theotokos. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2203]: Troparion 1 of Ode 4, Tone 8: Comprehensive ancestral commemoration of fathers, forefathers, grandfathers, and great-grandfathers fallen asleep in faith. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2204]: Troparion 2 of Ode 4, Tone 8: Commemoration of monastics and laymen, young and old, who passed away in mountains, journeys, or deserts. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2205]: Troparion 3 of Ode 4, Tone 8: Prayer for those who departed unexpectedly in midst of joy or grief, in prosperity or affliction. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2206]: Troparion 4 of Ode 4, Tone 8: Commemoration of victims of violence and accidents: sword, galloping horses, hail, snow, torrential rain, falling masonry, or landslides. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2207]: Triadicon of Ode 4 (*Glory*), Tone 8: The mystery of the three divine Hypostases worshipped as one undivided Godhead. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2208]: Theotokion of Ode 4 (*Both now*), Tone 8: The Virgin guiding souls battered by waves of sin safely into the spiritual port of salvation. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2209]: Ode 5 of the Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos (Isa. 26:9): Early morning seeking of God Whose commandments are divine light illuminating the mind. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2210]: Troparion 1 of Ode 5, Tone 8: Universal supplication for each departed soul from the beginning of time to be granted rest with the saints. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2211]: Troparion 2 of Ode 5, Tone 8: Prayer for rulers, princes, and monastics of the Orthodox faith to be delivered from eternal torment. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2212]: Troparion 3 of Ode 5, Tone 8: God Who foreknows what is profitable, besought to deliver from punishment all who died by sudden falls or unforeseen accidents. Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2213]: Troparion 4 of Ode 5, Tone 8: Supplication for deliverance from the unquenchable fire, outer darkness, gnashing of teeth, and the never-dying worm (Mk. 9:44). Physical Leaf p539 (printed p. 533).

[^2214]: Triadicon of Ode 5 (*Glory*), Tone 8: The co-enthroned, unoriginate Trinity uniting believers into the single desire of the divine commandments. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2215]: Theotokion of Ode 5 (*Both now*), Tone 8: Mary exalted above the fiery Seraphim, bearing Jesus the Savior Who deified human flesh. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2216]: Ode 6 of the Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos (Jonah 2): Cry of the soul caught in the belly of sins, pleading for salvation like the Prophet Jonah. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2217]: Troparion 1 of Ode 6, Tone 8: Christ the Prince of life Who loosed the pangs of death through His Passion, besought to give rest unto His departed servants. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2218]: Troparion 2 of Ode 6, Tone 8: Commemoration of those who perished by sorcery, potions, poisons, or choking upon bones. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2219]: Troparion 3 of Ode 6, Tone 8: Plea for mercy at the Last Judgment when all shall stand naked and exposed before the face of God. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2220]: Troparion 4 of Ode 6, Tone 8: The Archangel's final trumpet call awakening the dead unto the resurrection of life (1 Thess. 4:16). Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2221]: Troparion 5 of Ode 6, Tone 8: Prayer that all the faithful of every human race from the ages may glorify God eternally with His heavenly servants. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2222]: Triadicon of Ode 6 (*Glory*), Tone 8: Confession of the thrice-holy, co-enthroned Godhead holding and upholding all things by sovereign power. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2223]: Theotokion of Ode 6 (*Both now*), Tone 8: The Flower of life blossoming from the root of Jesse (Isa. 11:1), Christ God born of the pure Maiden. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2224]: Kontakion of the Departed, Tone 8 (*With the saints give rest*): Classical funeral kontakion beseeching rest where there is no pain, sorrow, nor sighing, but life everlasting. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2225]: Ikos of the Departed: Solemn meditation on mortality and the return of dust unto dust (Gen. 3:19), transforming funeral mourning into the song of Alleluia. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2226]: Ode 7 of the Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos (Song of the Three Holy Children, Dan. 3:26): God Who laid the foundations of the earth and established the heavens. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2227]: Troparion 1 of Ode 7, Tone 8: Performing the memory of the departed of all ages, praising the God of our fathers. Physical Leaf p540 (printed p. 534).

[^2228]: Troparion 2 of Ode 7 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Commemoration of those who met sudden or accidental deaths by falling from heights, trees, iron structures, or precipitous rocks, pleading for their eternal repose. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2229]: Troparion 3 of Ode 7 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Supplication for those who served Christ in orthodox faith during life to be placed at His right hand among the sheep at the dread Last Judgment (cf. Mt. 25:33). Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2230]: Troparion 4 of Ode 7 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Prayer for the departed servants of Christ to be numbered among the company of the elect, chanting the song of the Three Holy Children of Babylon (Song of the Three Holy Children, Dan. 3:26). Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2231]: Troparion 5 of Ode 7 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Reflection on God fashioning mortal flesh from earthly dust and breathing into it the breath of life (Gen. 2:7), beseeching unfading eternal life for the departed. The marginal letter *(T)* forms part of the Studite acrostic: "Of the unworthy Theodore." Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2232]: Triadicon of Ode 7 (*Glory*), Tone 8: Classic theological analogy comparing the Holy Trinity unto three suns united in a single blending of uncreated light, confessing one divine essence in three distinct Hypostases. The marginal letter *(S)* continues the acrostic. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2233]: Theotokion of Ode 7 (*Both now*), Tone 8: Praise of the Virgin Mary as the noetic Mountain of God (cf. Ps. 67:16-17 LXX; Hab. 3:3), in whom the Word took human flesh and spiritualized and deified mankind. The marginal letter *(D)* continues the acrostic. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2234]: Ode 8 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos: Moses upon Mount Horeb beholding the mystery of the unburnt bush as a prefiguration of the virgin birth of Christ (Ex. 3:2), commanding all creation to hymn the Lord unto all ages. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2235]: Troparion 1 of Ode 8 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Christ crushing the power and darkness of death by His glorious Resurrection from the tomb like the sun, making all who died in the faith sons of the Resurrection (cf. Lk. 20:36). Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2236]: Troparion 2 of Ode 8 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Christ as the Witness of hidden and secret thoughts, besought not to enter into strict reckoning with those who fell asleep in faith when all deeds are laid bare (cf. 1 Cor. 4:5). Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2237]: Troparion 3 of Ode 8 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Prophetic depiction of the dread Judgment Day, when the Archangel's trumpet gathers all from the four corners of the earth (Mt. 24:31) to stand before the throne of Christ. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2238]: Troparion 4 of Ode 8 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Intercession for all who suffered violent or unforeseen deaths—from sudden shocks, loud shouts, panic, blows, suffocation, or execution—granting them remission and peaceful rest. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2239]: Triadicon of Ode 8 (*Bless we the Lord*), Tone 8: Doxology to the unoriginate Monad in essence and Trinity in Persons: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, glorifying the timeless majesty of the Kingdom. The marginal letter *(O)* continues the acrostic. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2240]: Theotokion of Ode 8 (*Both now*), Tone 8: The Theotokos hailed as the mystical "sealed fountain" (Song of Songs 4:12) pouring forth the waters of immortality unto all the faithful. The marginal letter *(R)* completes the acrostic. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2241]: Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8, Heirmos: Magnification of the virginal childbearing of Mary prefigured to the Lawgiver Moses in fire and the bush on Sinai for the salvation of the faithful. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2242]: Troparion 1 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Petition for all who from the beginning of time have fallen asleep in faith and hope to dwell in the joy of the saints. Physical Leaf p541 (printed p. 535).

[^2243]: Troparion 2 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Prayer for those who perished under sudden manifestations of natural wrath—lightning bolts, thunder from heaven, earthquakes, or shipwrecks at sea. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2244]: Troparion 3 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Universal intercession spanning every human stage and condition: the aged, youth, infants, sucklings, men and women together, commending them to God's mercy. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2245]: Troparion 4 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Commemoration of those who died from poisonous bites, ferocious beasts, horse trampling, or homicide through strangulation and hanging. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2246]: Troparion 5 of Ode 9 of the Funeral Canon, Tone 8: Supplication for each departed believer by name from every generation to stand without condemnation before Christ at His glorious Second Coming. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2247]: Triadicon of Ode 9 (*Glory*), Tone 8: Dogmatic confession of the Holy Trinity as one Godhead possessing three distinct personal hypostatic properties (*tres personae, una essentia*). The marginal letter *(Th)* opens the concluding acrostic block. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2248]: Theotokion of Ode 9 (*Both now*), Tone 8: The transcendent paradox of the Incarnation, wherein the unwedded Virgin suckles the Nourisher of the universe and holds in her arms the Redeemer Who sustains all things. The marginal letter *(U/V)* concludes the acrostic signature of St. Theodore the Studite. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2249]: Exapostilarion for the Saturday of Souls, Tone 3 (Melodic model: *Having adorned the heavens with stars*): Exhortation to the faithful to commemorate all who have fallen asleep in Christ and keep mindful of the Great and Final Day of Judgment. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2250]: Theotokion of the Exapostilarion, Tone 3: Entreaty to the Mother of God as the sweetness of angels, joy of the afflicted, and protectress of all Christians, delivering souls from eternal torments. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2251]: First sticheron at the Praises (Lauds), Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Solemn meditation on the dread hour of death and the terrifying tribunal of the Master, where unquenchable fire, outer darkness, and gnashing of teeth await the condemned. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2252]: Second sticheron at the Praises, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Moving call to all the living to visit the graves and observe the fleeting vanity of bodily beauty, worldly wealth, and haughty ambition. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2253]: Third sticheron at the Praises, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Contemplation of the transitory nature of earthly power, depicting the monarch once seated upon an imperial throne and clad in royal purple now lying corrupting in a lowly grave. Physical Leaf p542 (printed p. 536).

[^2254]: Fourth sticheron at the Praises, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Despairing on account of her life*): Supplication to the Savior to gather all departed of every age, rank, and condition into the bosom of Abraham and places of heavenly refreshment. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).

[^2255]: Doxasticon at the Praises (*Glory*), Tone 2: Poetic reflection comparing mortal existence to a fading flower and passing shadow (cf. Ps. 102:15-16 LXX; Isa. 40:6-8), contrasting human frailty with the triumphant resurrection of all the dead at the archangelic trumpet. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).

[^2256]: Theotokion at the Praises (*Both now*), Tone 6: Comprehensive theological synthesis honoring the Prophets who foresaw Christ, the Apostles who enlightened the nations, the Martyrs who glorified God by their blood, and the Virgin Mother who intercedes for the departed. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).

[^2257]: First sticheron at the Aposticha of Matins, Tone 6 (Melodic model: *Having laid aside all earthly hope*): Supplication to the inexhaustible fountain of divine mercy to grant the departed an enduring inheritance in the land of the living, bought by the precious Blood of Christ. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).

[^2258]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 24:13 LXX: Commemoration of Christ's voluntary, life-giving death and descent into Hades, beseeching remission of sins and resurrectional hope for all deceased faithful. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).

[^2259]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 64:5 LXX: Confession of Christ's sovereign lordship over both the living and the dead (Rom. 14:9), beseeching Him as Creator to rest His servants in the splendors of the saints. Physical Leaf p543 (printed p. 537).

[^2260]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha (*Glory*), Tone 6 (spanning leaves p543-p544): Scriptural reflection on the ancestral fall of Adam in Eden through the poison of the serpent and the entry of universal mortality, overthrown by the incarnation and resurrection of the Savior. Physical Leaves p543-p544 (printed pp. 537-538).

[^2261]: Theotokion at the Aposticha (*Both now*), Tone 6: Pure dogmatic confession of the Theotokos containing and bearing God in two unconfused natures and one Hypostasis, entreating her to pray for the rest of the departed in unfading heavenly radiance. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).

[^2262]: Dismissal troparion for the departed (*O Thou Who with wisdom profound*) and its Theotokion (*Thee we have as a wall and a haven*), Tone 8, followed by the customary liturgical dismissal concluding the Saturday of Souls Matins. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).

[^2263]: Rubric for the Divine Liturgy of the Saturday of Souls prescribing the Typika (*Izobrazitelna*), marking the final liturgical commemoration of the dead before the great Pentecost festival. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).

[^2264]: Epistle pericopes for the Saturday of Souls: Acts pericope 51 (Acts 28:1-31, the concluding chapter of Acts describing Paul's preaching in Rome) followed by the funeral pericope 163 (1 Cor. 15:47-57, the second Adam and the victory over death). Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).

[^2265]: Alleluiarion in Tone 6 with psalm verse Psalm 64:5 LXX (*Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen*), followed by Gospel pericopes: John 67 (Jn. 21:15-25, Christ's dialogue with Peter by the Sea of Tiberias) and funeral pericope 21 (Jn. 5:24-30, resurrection of the dead). Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).

[^2266]: Communion verse (Communion Hymn) for the departed from Psalm 64:5 LXX with threefold Alleluia, sealing the funeral rites of the Saturday of Souls before Pentecost. Physical Leaf p544 (printed p. 538).

[^2267]: Major Part VII Landmark opening the celebration of the Great Feast of Holy Pentecost (Leaves 545-668 / Printed pp. 539-663). The 1907 Lviv edition introduces this solemnity with an elaborate woodcut headpiece depicting the Mother of God and the Holy Apostles in the Cenacle receiving the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2268]: Rubric for Little Vespers on Saturday evening before Pentecost. The printed rubrical cinnabar text erroneously specifies "воскрⷭ҇ны" (resurrectional) stichera, an obvious typographical slip by the Stauropegion compositors, as the four stichera that follow are exclusively festal idiomela/prosomoia of Pentecost. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2269]: First sticheron at *Lord, I have cried* of Little Vespers, Tone 1 (Melodic model: *Joy of the heavenly ranks*): Proclamation of the renewal of all nations through the fiery tongues, empowering the disciples to declare the mighty works of God (Acts 2:11). Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2270]: Second sticheron at *Lord, I have cried* of Little Vespers, Tone 1 (Melodic model: *Joy of the heavenly ranks*): Christ fulfilling His solemn promise (Lk. 24:49) by sending down power from on high upon the Disciples through the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2271]: Third sticheron at *Lord, I have cried* of Little Vespers, Tone 1 (Melodic model: *Joy of the heavenly ranks*): The Comforter sent into the world in tongues of fire to consume human sin and restore genuine mystical communion with God. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2272]: Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried* (*Glory, both now*), Tone 7: Doxology venerating the Holy Spirit as "another Comforter" (Jn. 14:16) sent by Christ from the Father unto the earth. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2273]: First sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, Tone 2 (Melodic model: *O House of Ephratha*): The minds of the Disciples illuminated by divine radiance as they receive the Holy Spirit essentially into their inner being. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2274]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, Tone 2, with psalm verse Psalm 50:12 LXX: The descent of the divided tongues of fire resting upon the Apostles, enlightening without burning. Physical Leaf p545 (printed p. 539).

[^2275]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha of Little Vespers, Tone 2, with psalm verse Psalm 50:13 LXX: Prayer to the heavenly Comforter to sanctify and preserve all who proclaim Him as God. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2276]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Little Vespers (*Glory, both now*), Tone 1: The celebrated classical Byzantine pneumatological hymn detailing the gifts of the Holy Spirit: inspiring prophecies, ordaining priests, granting wisdom to fishermen, and convening the Church. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2277]: Troparion (Dismissal Troparion) of the Great Feast of Pentecost in Tone 8 (*Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God*): The central festal hymn glorifying Christ for revealing fishermen as all-wise through the descent of the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2278]: Service opening of Great Vespers for the Great Feast of Holy Pentecost, celebrated on Saturday evening. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2279]: Rubric governing the stichologia of *Lord, I have cried* at Great Vespers, prescribing 10 stichera: 3 idiomela in Tone 1 repeated, followed by 5 idiomela in Tone 2 with repetitions to complete the decade. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2280]: First festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1 (sung twice): Solemn theological celebration of Pentecost as the realization of divine promise, completion of Christian hope, and revelation of the great Trinitarian mystery. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2281]: Second festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: Christ renewing the Disciples with the gift of foreign tongues so they may preach the immortal Word throughout all the nations. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2282]: Third festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 1: Repetition of the grand dogmatic hymn on the operations of the Holy Spirit in the hierarchy, wisdom, and universal assembly of the Church. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2283]: Fourth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2 (sung twice): The famous liturgical confession (*We have seen the true Light*), chanted throughout the Byzantine Rite following Holy Communion, originating historically as a Pentecost sticheron. Physical Leaf p546 (printed p. 540).

[^2284]: Fifth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: The historical continuity of divine revelation, from the prefigurations of the Old Testament Prophets to the outpouring of grace upon the Apostles. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2285]: Sixth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: The personal offering of prayer and worship with genuflection before the invincible power of Christ at morning, midday, and evening (Ps. 54:18 LXX). Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2286]: Seventh festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: Corporate kneeling of souls and bodies by the assembly in the temple courts, praising the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit as one co-eternal God. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2287]: Eighth festal idiomelon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 2: Confession of the Consubstantial Trinity as proclaimed harmoniously by all Prophets, Apostles, and Holy Martyrs. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2288]: Festal Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried* (*Glory, both now*), Tone 8, attributed to the Byzantine Emperor Leo VI the Wise (886–912): The monumental dogmatic anthem proclaiming the Thrice-Hypostatic Godhead and interpreting the Trisagion (*Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal*) in light of the three divine Persons. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2289]: Rubrics for the solemn Entrance (*Vkhod*) with incense, the ancient evening hymn *Phos Hilaron* (*O Joyful Light*), the Saturday evening Prokeimenon, and the three Old Testament Paroemia readings of Pentecost. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2290]: First Paroemia of Pentecost: Numbers 11:16-17, 24-29, recounting God placing the prophetic Spirit upon the seventy elders of Israel and Moses' memorable wish that all the Lord's people were prophets. Physical Leaf p547 (printed p. 541).

[^2291]: Second Paroemia of Pentecost: Joel 2:23-32, the principal Old Testament prophecy of the outpouring of the Holy Spirit upon all flesh, cited by St. Peter on the morning of Pentecost (Acts 2:16-21). Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).

[^2292]: Third Paroemia of Pentecost: Ezekiel 36:24-28, the prophetic promise of the New Covenant, in which God takes away the heart of stone, bestows a new heart of flesh, and places His Holy Spirit within His people. Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).

[^2293]: Stichera at the Litija of Pentecost, Tone 2: Three solemn idiomela celebrating the manifestation of the Holy Spirit in the Prophets, the bowing of knees in the courts of the Lord, and praise of the Co-unoriginate Trinity. Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).

[^2294]: Doxasticon at the Litija (*Glory, both now*), Tone 8: Graphic hymnographic depiction of the amazement of the crowd in Jerusalem hearing the unlearned Galilean disciples speak mysteries in foreign tongues. Physical Leaf p548 (printed p. 542).

[^2295]: First sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 6: The astonishment and mockery of the onlookers mistaking the divine intoxication of the Holy Spirit for worldly drunkenness (Acts 2:13), countered by the steadfast prayer of the faithful. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).

[^2296]: Second sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 50:12 LXX: The descent of the Spirit as an incomprehensible wonder transforming fishermen into heralds of salvation. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).

[^2297]: Third sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 6, with psalm verse Psalm 50:13 LXX: The venerable prayer to the Holy Spirit (*O Heavenly King, O Comforter*), integral to the opening prayers of every Byzantine liturgical service, originating as a festal Aposticha sticheron for Pentecost. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).

[^2298]: Doxasticon at the Aposticha of Great Vespers (*Glory, both now*), Tone 8: Masterpiece of Byzantine hymnographic typology contrasting the division and confusion of tongues at the Tower of Babel (Gen. 11:7-9) with the unifying harmony of tongues at Pentecost. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).

[^2299]: Dismissal Troparion of Pentecost, Tone 8 (*Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God*), chanted three times during the Artoklasia (blessing of the five loaves, wheat, wine, and oil) at the conclusion of Great Vespers. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).

[^2300]: Codicological and Typikon rubrics specifying the all-night vigil reading from the Acts of the Apostles, the order for celebrations without a vigil, Compline propers, Midnight Office, and the Sunday Triadicon Canon of the Octoechos. Physical Leaf p549 (printed p. 543).

[^2301]: Service opening of Sunday Matins for the Great Feast of Holy Pentecost, introduced with a floral engraved headpiece band. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).

[^2302]: First Sessional Hymn after the first Kathisma stichology at Matins, Tone 4 (Melodic model: *Joseph was amazed*): Veneration of Pentecost as the ultimate feast and fulfillment of divine promises, illuminating the disciples as mystagogues of the heavenly mysteries. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).

[^2303]: Rubric prescribing the patristic reading from St. John Chrysostom's Homilies on the Gospel of John (Homily 50 on John 7:37: "In the last day, that great day of the feast..."), divided into two sections. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).

[^2304]: Second Sessional Hymn after the second Kathisma stichology at Matins, Tone 4 (Melodic model: *Joseph was amazed*): The mystical Fountain of the Spirit descending as rivers of fire and a bedewing cloud upon the Apostles, bestowing regeneration through fire and water. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).

[^2305]: Polyeleos and Megalynarion (Praise) of Holy Pentecost: The assembly's solemn chant honoring Christ the Life-Giver and the Most Holy Spirit sent forth from the Father upon the holy Disciples. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).

[^2306]: Selected Psalm verses chanted during the Megalynarion of Pentecost, drawing upon Psalm 18:2, 32:6, 96:3, 103:30, 142:10, 50:12, 50:13, and 28:11 LXX to glorify the cosmic creative and sanctifying work of the Holy Spirit. Physical Leaf p550 (printed p. 544).

[^2307]: The rubric *И҆ чте́нїе въ Бг҃осло́вѣ* appoints a patristic reading from the homilies of St. Gregory the Theologian (Nazianzen), specifically Oration 41 (*On Pentecost*), appointed for the festal Matins vigil.

[^2308]: Melodic model heading: *Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded secretly" (Повелѣ́нное та́йно)* matches Byzantine Greek *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*.

[^2309]: Sessional hymn after the Polyeleos praising Christ's Resurrection, Ascension, and the sending of the Holy Spirit upon the Apostles as the mystical plectrum upon a musical harp (*ꙗ҆́кѡ гꙋ́сль мꙋсікі́йскаѧ*).

[^2310]: Second rubric appointing the continuation of the homily of St. Gregory the Theologian between the Sessional Hymn and the Anabathmoi.

[^2311]: Songs of Ascents (Anabathmoi): The first antiphon of Tone 4 (*Ѿ ю҆́ности моеѧ̀* / *From my youth*), appointed universally on great feasts of the Master.

[^2312]: Festal Matins Prokeimenon: Psalm 142:10 (LXX) (*Дх҃ъ тво́й бл҃гі́й наста́витъ мѧ̀ на зе́млю пра́вꙋ*).

[^2313]: Prokeimenon verse: Psalm 142:1 (LXX).

[^2314]: Pentecost Matins Gospel: John 20:19-23 (pericope 65), recounting Christ's appearance to the disciples in the Upper Room on the evening of the Resurrection, breathing on them and giving the Holy Spirit for the forgiveness of sins.

[^2315]: Liturgical rubric: The resurrectional hymn *Воскресе́нїе Хрїсто́во ви́дѣвше* (*Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ*) is omitted on Pentecost despite it being a Sunday, because the feast commemorates the descent of the Holy Spirit rather than the weekly resurrectional cycle. Psalm 50 is read immediately.

[^2316]: Post-Gospel idiomelon: Tone 6, *Царю́ небе́сный* (*O Heavenly King*), sung in place of the customary Sunday post-Gospel sticheron *Воскре́съ І҆исоу́съ ѿ гро́ба*.

[^2317]: Canon performance rubric: The two festal canons are chanted with their heirmoi doubled (sung twice each) by the alternating right and left choirs, and their troparia repeated to make 12.

[^2318]: Authorship and acrostic of the First Canon: Attributed to St. Cosmas the Melodist (Bishop of Maiuma, c. 675–c. 752); the Greek acrostic *Πεντηκοστὴν ἑορτάζομεν* forms an alphabet of praises for Pentecost.

[^2319]: Canon 1, Ode 1 Heirmos: Tone 7, referencing the defeat of Pharaoh in the Red Sea (Exod. 14:27-28; 15:1-19).

[^2320]: Canon 1, Ode 1, 1st troparion: Christ fulfilling the promise of sending the Comforter unto the Apostles.

[^2321]: Canon 1, Ode 1, 2nd troparion: Fulfillment of the Old Testament prophecies regarding the outpouring of the Holy Spirit (cf. Joel 2:28).

[^2322]: Canon 2 (Iambic) Acrostic: A four-line iambic trimeter dedicatory poem printed in both Greek and Church Slavonic diplomatic verse, dedicating the canon to the Word born of God and the Comforter.

[^2323]: Authorship of the Second Canon: Attributed in the Slavic tradition to *Кѵ́ръ І҆ѡа́ннъ А҆ркалі́йскїй* (St. John of Damascus, c. 676–749), distinguished by its classical Greek iambic meter.

[^2324]: Canon 2, Ode 1 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, referencing Moses veiled in the divine cloud on Mount Sinai receiving the Law (Exod. 19–20; 24:15-18).

[^2325]: Canon 2, Ode 1, 1st troparion: Christ promising that He will pour out ungrudging grace upon His friends from the Father's throne.

[^2326]: Canon 2, Ode 1, 2nd troparion: The descent of the Spirit in rushing wind and tongues of fire fulfilling the promise of Christ.

[^2327]: Katavasia rubric: Both heirmoi of Ode 1 (Cosmas's *He Who broke battles* and John's *Covered by the divine darkness*) are sung by the choirs.

[^2328]: Canon 1, Ode 3 Heirmos: Tone 7, Christ commanding the disciples to abide in Jerusalem until clothed with power from on high (Luke 24:49; Acts 1:4-8).

[^2329]: Canon 1, Ode 3, 1st troparion: The contrast between the division of tongues at Babel (Gen. 11:7-9) and their divine unification at Pentecost.

[^2330]: Canon 2, Ode 3 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, referencing the prayer of the Prophetess Hannah (1 Sam. 2:1-10; 1 Kgs. 2:1-10 LXX).

[^2331]: Canon 2, Ode 3, 1st troparion: Incomprehensible wisdom of God making unlettered fishermen eloquent theologians.

[^2332]: Canon 2, Ode 3, 2nd troparion: The Holy Spirit as Light proceeding from the Unbegotten Light of the Father, revealed through the Son.

[^2333]: Sessional Hymn after Ode 3: Tone 8, *Повелѣ́нное та́йно*, praising the descent of the tongues of fire upon the Apostles in the Upper Room.

[^2334]: Canon 1, Ode 4 Heirmos: Tone 7, citing Habakkuk 3:2 (LXX) regarding the saving coming of Christ.

[^2335]: Canon 1, Ode 4, 1st troparion: The Holy Spirit speaking in the Prophets and now revealed to the Apostles.

[^2336]: Canon 1, Ode 4, 2nd troparion: The division of tongues of fire as the sovereign manifestation of the Father's power.

[^2337]: Canon 2, Ode 4 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, addressing Christ as King of kings and the proceeder from the Uncaused Father, sending the equal Spirit.

[^2338]: Canon 2, Ode 4, 1st troparion: The bath of regeneration flowing from Christ's pierced side (John 19:34; Titus 3:5), sealed with the warmth of the Spirit.

[^2339]: Canon 2, Ode 4, 2nd troparion: Worship of the Three Persons in one Essence at the outpouring of the Spirit.

[^2340]: Canon 2, Ode 4, 3rd troparion: Ministers of the Trinity illuminated with divine fire unto salvation.

[^2341]: Canon 1, Ode 5 Heirmos: Tone 7, citing Isaiah 26:18-19 (LXX) regarding the conception of the spirit of salvation.

[^2342]: Canon 1, Ode 5, 1st troparion: The Good Spirit of wisdom proceeding from the Father and revealed through the Son.

[^2343]: Canon 2, Ode 5 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, referencing the fire-inspired dew of the Spirit and the law going forth from Zion (Isa. 2:3).

[^2344]: Canon 2, Ode 5, 1st troparion: The Spirit proceeding unmastered from the Father and sealing the teaching of the Savior.

[^2345]: Canon 2, Ode 5, 2nd troparion: The Word preparing the minds of the Apostles as a clean dwelling for the Light of the Spirit.

[^2346]: Canon 1, Ode 6 Heirmos: Tone 7, the typological prayer of Jonah from the belly of the sea monster (Jonah 2:1-10).

[^2347]: Canon 1, Ode 6, 1st troparion: Outpouring of the Spirit upon all flesh (Joel 2:28; Acts 2:17).

[^2348]: Canon 2, Ode 6 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, Christ rising from the Virgin and snatching fallen Adam from corruption as Jonah was snatched from the sea beast.

[^2349]: Canon 2, Ode 6, 1st troparion: Supplication for the Holy Spirit to burn up the defilements of the adversary and cleanse the mind.

[^2350]: Canon 2, Ode 6, 2nd troparion: The Holy Spirit confirming the Apostles in Zion with fire-breathed inspiration.

[^2351]: Festal Kontakion: Tone 8, *Є҆гда̀ снизше́дъ ꙗ҆зы́ки слїѧ̀* (*When the Most High came down and confused the tongues*), contrasting the Tower of Babel (Gen. 11) with the unity of Pentecost (Acts 2).

[^2352]: Festal Ikos: Prayer unto Jesus for swift consolation and the indwelling presence of the All-Holy Spirit.

[^2353]: Synaxarion of Holy Pentecost: The official liturgical commentary on the historical and theological meaning of the fiftieth day.

[^2354]: Synaxarion commentary on the Jewish origins of the Feast of Weeks (Shavuot, Exod. 23:16; 34:22; Lev. 23:15-21; Deut. 16:9-12).

[^2355]: Synaxarion exposition of the three great pilgrimage feasts of Israel: Passover (Pesach), Pentecost (Shavuot), and the Feast of Booths / Tabernacles (Sukkot / Scenopegia, Lev. 23:33-43; Deut. 16:13-16).

[^2356]: Synaxarion exposition of the mystical significance of the number seven and the Jubilee year (*Їѡвнле́онъ*, Lev. 25:8-55) releasing debts and slaves every 50th year.

[^2357]: Typological interpretation of Scenopegia and the Feast of Tabernacles as an image of the general Resurrection and the reconstructed human tabernacle.

[^2358]: Patristic decision to dedicate the Monday of the Holy Spirit to the Third Person of the Trinity specifically, while Sunday honors the descent of the Spirit in the economy of the Trinity.

[^2359]: Canon 1, Ode 7 Heirmos: Tone 7, citing Daniel 3:26-56 (the Three Holy Youths in the Babylonian furnace).

[^2360]: Canon 1, Ode 7, 1st troparion: The mocking accusation of drunkenness (Acts 2:13) refuted by the preaching of the Trinity.

[^2361]: Canon 1, Ode 7, 2nd troparion: Orthodox theological confession of the Father, Word, and Spirit as one undivided dominion.

[^2362]: Canon 2, Ode 7 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, the music of Nebuchadnezzar's orchestra (Dan. 3:5-7) contrasted with the harmonious praise of the Holy Trinity.

[^2363]: Canon 2, Ode 7, 1st troparion: Refutation of the foolish ones who mistook apostolic ecstasy for wine.

[^2364]: Canon 2, Ode 7, 2nd troparion: The prophecy of Joel 2:28 (*I will pour out of My Spirit*) fulfilled at Pentecost.

[^2365]: Canon 2, Ode 7, 3rd troparion: The threefold grace revealing the Three Hypostases on the Lord's Day.

[^2366]: Canon 1, Ode 8 Heirmos: Tone 7, the unburned bush of Sinai (Exod. 3:2) and the three youths in the furnace (Dan. 3:57).

[^2367]: Canon 1, Ode 8, 1st troparion: The rushing blast from on high inspiring the fishermen to proclaim the wonders of God.

[^2368]: Canon 1, Ode 8, 2nd troparion: Spiritual ascent unto Mount Zion and the city of the Living God (Heb. 12:22).

[^2369]: Canon 2, Ode 8 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, the Threefold image of the Godhead bedewing the flame of the furnace.

[^2370]: Canon 2, Ode 8, 1st troparion: The Holy Spirit bringing to remembrance all the saving words of Christ (John 14:26).

[^2371]: Canon 2, Ode 8, 2nd troparion: Christ as the self-shining Light filling the Apostles with the bountiful Spirit.

[^2372]: Canon 2, Ode 8, 3rd troparion: The Spirit proceeding from the Father's bosom and sharing the eternal throne with the Incarnate Word.

[^2373]: Festal Refrain of Ode 9: *Велича́й дꙋшѐ моѧ̀, ѿ Ѻ҆ц҃а̀ и҆ Сн҃а и҆сходѧ́щаго ст҃а́го Дх҃а* (*Magnify, O my soul, the Holy Spirit, Who proceeds from the Father and the Son*). Note the historical Ruthenian / Uniate recension reading incorporating the *Filioque* clause, standard in the 1907 Lviv Stauropegion edition pursuant to the Union of Brest and Roman liturgical confirmations for the Greek Catholic Church.

[^2374]: Canon 1, Ode 9 Heirmos: Tone 7, hymn of praise to the Virgin Theotokos who brought forth the Word without corruption.

[^2375]: Canon 1, Ode 9, 1st troparion: The ascent of Elijah in the fiery chariot (2 Kgs. 2:11; 4 Kgs. 2:11 LXX) prefiguring the descent of the fiery breath upon the Apostles.

[^2376]: Canon 1, Ode 9, 2nd troparion: The miracle of Pentecost where people of diverse nations understood the one apostolic preaching (Acts 2:6-11).

[^2377]: Second Festal Refrain of Ode 9: *Велича́й дꙋшѐ моѧ̀, въ трїе́хъ ли́цѣхъ сꙋ́щее є҆ди́но Бж҃ество̀* (*Magnify, O my soul, the One Godhead existing in three Persons*).

[^2378]: Canon 2, Ode 9 Heirmos: Tone 4 iambic, *Ра́дꙋйсѧ Цр҃и́це* (*Rejoice, O Queen*), praising the Mother of God whose childbearing surpasses all human eloquence.

[^2379]: Canon 2, Ode 9, 1st troparion: The Virgin Mary bearing the healing Word Who now, seated at the right hand of the Father, sends the grace of the Spirit.

[^2380]: Canon 2, Ode 9, 2nd troparion: The transformation of believers through the outbreathing of God-flowing grace.

[^2381]: Katavasia rubric: Chanting both Heirmoi of Ode 9 together.

[^2382]: Melodic model heading: *Tone 3, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми)*.

[^2383]: Festal Exapostilarion 1: Citing the Holy Spirit proceeding from the Father and sent through the Son to the unlettered disciples.

[^2384]: Festal Exapostilarion 2: Trinitarian hymn proclaiming the Father, Word, and Spirit as uncreated Light.

[^2385]: Praises (Lauds) 1st Idiomelon: Tone 2, St. Luke's account of the descent of the Spirit in Acts 2:1-4.

[^2386]: Praises 2nd Idiomelon: Tone 2, dogmatic confession of the eternal, uncreated nature of the Holy Spirit, co-ranked with the Father and the Son.

[^2387]: Praises 3rd Idiomelon: Tone 2, the Holy Spirit as Light, Life, and Noetic Fountain, crowning prophets, apostles, and martyrs.

[^2388]: Post-Praises Doxasticon: Tone 6, the foundational invocation of the Holy Spirit, *Царю́ небе́сный* (*O Heavenly King*).

[^2389]: Festal Matins Dismissal: The proper dismissal formula for Pentecost mentioning the sending of the Holy Spirit in the form of fiery tongues.

[^2390]: First Hour rubric: Appointing the troparion and kontakion of Pentecost, followed by the final dismissal.

[^2391]: Divine Liturgy Typikon rubric: The festal antiphons of Pentecost replace the customary daily or Sunday antiphons.

[^2392]: Liturgy Antiphon 1: Psalm 18:2, 3, 5 (LXX) with the refrain *Through the intercessions of the Theotokos...*

[^2393]: Liturgy Antiphon 2: Psalm 19:2, 3, 5 (LXX) with the proper festal refrain *Save us, O Good Comforter, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia*.

[^2394]: Liturgy Antiphon 2 conclusion: Chanting the Christological hymn *Only-Begotten Son and Immortal Word of God* (*Е҆диноро́дный Сы́не*).

[^2395]: Liturgy Antiphon 3: Psalm 20:2, 3, 4 (LXX) with the festal troparion *Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God...*

[^2396]: Festal Troparion of Pentecost: Tone 8, *Бл҃гослове́нъ є҆сѝ Хрⷭ҇тѐ Бж҃е на́шъ* (*Blessed art Thou, O Christ our God, Who hast revealed the fishermen to be all-wise...*).

[^2397]: Liturgy Little Entrance Verse (Introit / Eisodikon): Psalm 20:14 (LXX) (*Вознеси́сѧ Гдⷭ҇и си́лою твое́ю*).

[^2398]: Liturgy Festal Kontakion: Tone 8, *Є҆гда̀ снизше́дъ ꙗ҆зы́ки слїѧ̀*.

[^2399]: Baptismal Hymn instead of Trisagion: Galatians 3:27 (*Є҆ли́цы во Хрⷭ҇та̀ крⷭ҇ти́стесѧ*), sung on great feasts that were historically baptismal synaxes.

[^2400]: Liturgy Prokeimenon: Tone 8, Psalm 18:5, 2 (LXX), sung daily through the Apodosis of Pentecost.

[^2401]: Liturgy Epistle: Acts 2:1-11 (pericope 3), St. Peter and the Apostles at Pentecost.

[^2402]: Liturgy Alleluia: Tone 1, Psalm 32:6, 13 (LXX).

[^2403]: Liturgy Gospel: John 7:37-52; 8:12 (pericope 27), Christ preaching rivers of living water flowing from the believer's belly on the last great day of the Feast of Tabernacles.

[^2404]: Liturgy Megalynarion replacement: The Heirmos of Ode 9 of the Iambic Canon (*Ра́дꙋйсѧ Цр҃и́це* / *Rejoice, O Queen*) is sung in place of *It is truly meet* (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*) until the Apodosis.

[^2405]: Liturgy Communion Verse (Communion Hymn): Psalm 142:10 (LXX) (*Дх҃ъ тво́й бл҃гі́й наста́витъ мѧ̀ на зе́млю пра́вꙋ*).

[^2406]: Liturgy conclusion rubric: The Ninth Hour is read after the dismissal of the Liturgy, preceding the immediate celebration of Pentecost Kneeling Vespers.

[^2407]: Sunday of Pentecost Kneeling Vespers (*Вечерня с коленопреклонением*): Celebrated immediately after the Divine Liturgy or in the afternoon, during which the Church kneels for the first time since Pascha and offers the seven solemn Kneeling Prayers.

[^2408]: Rubric on the fast ringing of bells: *Зна́менаетъ ско́рѡ* indicates a rapid peal of the church bells to call the faithful to the service of the Kneeling Prayers.

[^2409]: Vespers Opening: The priest intones the initial blessing, the reader chants *O Heavenly King* (solemnly returning to the beginning of services), and the 103rd Psalm (LXX) is read.

[^2410]: Great Litany (*Вели́каѧ є҆кте́нїа*): Intoned with special Pentecostal petitions added after the customary supplications.

[^2411]: Hierarchical commemoration: Standard Ruthenian formulary commemorating the Metropolitan Archbishop of Lviv (Andrey Sheptytsky, 1865–1944) and the local Eparchial Bishop (e.g. Hryhoriy Khomyshyn of Stanyslaviv or Kostiantyn Chekhovych of Peremyshl).

[^2412]: State petition: Diplomatic commemoration for Emperor Franz Joseph I of Austria-Hungary (*Бл҃говѣ́рный І҆мпера́торъ*), King of Galicia and Lodomeria, together with his Imperial Court (*Пала́та*) and the Armed Forces (*Во́и*).

[^2413]: Special Pentecost Vespers Petitions: A series of twelve unique petitions invoking the grace and illumination of the Holy Spirit, preparation for kneeling, deliverance from demonic bonds, and divine sanctification.

[^2414]: Commemoration of the Theotokos concluding the petitions of the Great Litany before the priest's exclamation.

[^2415]: Printed folio 555 (фн҃є). Concluding exclamation of the Great Litany of Pentecost Vespers (*Я́кѡ подоба́етъ тебѣ̀ всѧ́каѧ сла́ва...*), completing the extensive kneeling-eve litany begun on leaf p559 and continued on p560.

[^2416]: Rubric for the Lucernarium psalms (*Го́споди воззва́хъ* / Ps. 140 LXX) with 6 appointed stichos-verses.

[^2417]: Idiomelic stichera (*стїхи̑ры самогла́сны*) for Pentecost Sunday Vespers, Tone 4.

[^2418]: The first idiomelic sticheron (*Пресла́внаѧ дне́сь, ви́дѣша всѝ ꙗ҆зы́цы...*), celebrating the descent of the Holy Spirit upon the Apostles as recorded in Acts 2:1-4.

[^2419]: Incipit of the second idiomelic sticheron (*Дх҃ъ ст҃ы́й бѣ̀ оу҆́бѡ при́снѡ...*), sung to complete the 6 verses by repetition.

[^2420]: Incipit of the third idiomelic sticheron (*Дх҃ъ ст҃ы́й, Свѣ́тъ, и҆ Живо́тъ...*).

[^2421]: Rubric indicating that the three idiomelic stichera are repeated to fill out the 6 stichoi (*Повторя́юще ꙗ҆̀*).

[^2422]: Doxasticon at *Lord, I have cried*, Tone 6 (*Цр҃ю̀ нбⷭ҇ный, Оу҆тѣ́шителю...*), invoking the Paraclete.

[^2423]: Cross-reference rubric (*Пи́саны вы́ше на хвали́техъ*): this sticheron was previously appointed and printed in full at the Praises of Pentecost Matins (fol. 554 / physical leaf p560).

[^2424]: Rubric for the Little Entrance with the censer (*Вхо́дъ съ кади́ломъ*), customary at Great Vespers.

[^2425]: The ancient Lucernarium hymn *O Gladsome Light* (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй* / Phos Hilaron).

[^2426]: Great Prokeimenon (*Прокі́менъ вели́кїй*), Tone 7.

[^2427]: Psalm 76:14 LXX: *Who is a god great as our God? Thou art the God Who workest wonders* (*Кто̀ Бг҃ъ ве́лїй ꙗ҆́кѡ Бг҃ъ на́шъ; ты̀ є҆сѝ Бг҃ъ творѧ́й чꙋдеса̀*).

[^2428]: Psalm 76:15 LXX: *Thou hast made Thy power known among the peoples* (*Сказа́лъ є҆сѝ въ лю́дехъ си́лу тво́ю*).

[^2429]: Psalm 76:11 LXX: *And I said: Now have I begun; this is the change of the right hand of the Most High* (*И҆ рѣ́хъ: ны́нѣ нача́хъ, сїѧ̀ и҆змѣ́на десни́цы вы́шнѧгѡ*).

[^2430]: Psalm 76:12 LXX: *I remembered the works of the Lord; for I will remember Thy wonders from the beginning* (*Помѧнꙋ́хъ дѣла̀ Гдⷭ҇нѧ, ꙗ҆́кѡ помѧнꙋ̀ ѿ нача́ла чꙋдеса̀ твоѧ̑*).

[^2431]: Rubric introducing the first station of the Pentecost Kneeling Prayers (*Колѣ́нопрекло́нныѧ моли́твы*).

[^2432]: Diaconal bidding commanding the congregation to kneel (*Па́ки и҆ па́ки прикло́нше колѣ́на...*), marking the solemn return of bodily genuflection after the fifty-day paschal prohibition.
